市場焦點
美國
• 10月S&P Global綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖較前月回落，但仍處高檔區間；服務業動
能略為放緩，製造業PMI則升至56.0，創近4年半新高。新訂單增速加快，顯示企業需求、投
資及庫存回補動能仍具韌性，然而企業售價與投入成本同步上升，反映需求改善、供應鏈延
遲及成本轉嫁效應持續存在，通膨降溫進程仍具不確定性。整體而言，市場關注焦點轉向通
膨與利率走勢，高利率環境恐維持較長時間，進而推高股債資產評價波動。
中國
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期，在出口與科技製造業帶動下，
短期成長可望維持穩定，但若內需未能有效改善，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散，建議聚焦在
半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈，及高端製造相關產業，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股，

仍需觀察後續刺激政策與消費信心回升情況。
法國
政局動盪
• 法國金融市場因各地抗議活動持續擴大，面臨股債雙跌的局面。市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支
出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴
大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標仍處於歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐
元區主權債務危機期間，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務危機的風險仍相對有限。

美國PMI維持強勁擴張，經濟成長續航但通膨壓力再升溫
• 企業需求強勁支撐美國景氣維持高檔擴張：美國10月綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖略低於前月，但仍處於近五年來
的高位區間，顯示企業活動持續高速擴張；其中服務業PMI為57.9、製造業PMI升至56.0並創53個月新高，新訂單增速
更創逾4年半最佳表現，反映內需穩健、出口需求改善與企業補庫存效應同步發酵，美國第四季經濟動能仍具韌性。
• 成本上升與供應瓶頸推升通膨風險：企業售價漲幅創2022年8月以來最高，能源、運輸、金屬原料及薪資成本同步上揚，
加上供應鏈交期延長與訂單積壓擴大，使企業得以持續轉嫁成本，暗示未來數月消費者通膨可能再度偏離聯準會目標區
間，市場對利率維持較高水準的預期恐因此升溫。
• 後市觀察：景氣韌性有利獲利成長，但通膨可能推升殖利率、壓抑高評價資產。投資配置建議優先布局美國大型科技股、

美國金融股及工業資本支出受惠族群，可透過NASDAQ 100 ETF、S&P 500 ETF及美國銀行股ETF參與企業獲利成長；
固定收益則以短天期投資級債為主，以降低利率波動風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.24
S&P Global PMI 走勢

海外資金流入美國市場，支撐美國資產吸引力
• 海外資金持續流入美國：美國財政部8月TIC資料顯示，
整體資金淨流入1,998億美元，其中民間部門淨流入
1,785億美元，為主要資金來源；外資淨買進美國長
期證券3,007億美元，遠高於美國投資人加碼海外資
產216億美元，反映全球資金在經濟成長與流動性考
量下，對美國金融市場仍維持高度的配置需求。
• 全球資金偏好美國資產：外資8月淨買入美國股票
1,319億美元，高於美國公債與公司債，顯示海外投
資人仍看好美國企業獲利與經濟基本面，雖然短期國
庫券持有量下降，部分跨境美元資金亦出現波動，但
以10月近況來看長天期公債標售仍吸引境外央行、
保險資金及大型資產管理機構參與，反映高殖利率水
準對長期資金仍具吸引力。在經濟成長維持韌性與企

業獲利支撐下，美國資產仍為全球資金配置的重要核
心市場。
• 市場看法：海外資金流入有助反映美國資產吸引力，
惟後續仍須觀察通膨、Fed政策及企業獲利表現。投
資布局建議可以美國投資等級債、美國中短天期公債
及大型AI基礎建設受惠族群為核心配置，兼顧收益、
防禦及長期成長潛力。

顧
歷史數據顯示全球股市突破歷史新高後，後續上漲機率高
• 很多人直覺以為見高就是頂，但過去20年的統計資料顯示，全球股市在創下新高後，多數時間選擇繼續向上走。
• 勝率顯著偏高：全球股市(MSCI全球指數)創新高後持有一年，獲得正報酬的機率高達62%，三個月後的正報酬機率更達
71%。美股與台股表現最亮眼，標普500指數創高後一年上漲機率為76%，那斯達克為79%；而台灣股市表現更為驚人，創
高後三個月內上漲的機率高達82%。
• 下檔拉回風險有限：在創高後短期(一個月內)修正幅度其實相當溫和，全球多數主要市場的回徹幅度中位數僅1%～2%左右。
• 突破前高優於連續創新高：若市場在歷經至少一個月以上的整理後突破前高，其未來一年的平均報酬率高達8.4%，遠高於
連續每天創新高的3.9%。這代表市場經適度整理後的突破，具備更健康的上漲動能。

創新高後的平均遠期報酬率
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22

顧
市場寬度收窄，由少數巨頭獨撐
• 當前的市場特徵由少數巨頭帶頭衝：科技股權重較高的指數(如那斯達克、台股、韓股)，長期以來都有少數權重股拉動指數
的現象。自2024年以來，全球13個主要市場中有10個市場在創新高時，參與上漲的股票比例明顯下降。已開發市場創新高
時高於200日均線的股票比例從過去75.5%降至65.1%，新興市場更降至61.3%。這意味著指數看似風光，但多數個股並未
同步創高，當漲勢過度集中於少數大型科技巨頭時，整體市場的抗風險能力會相對減弱。
• 小結與投資建議：歷史經驗顯示，創新高本身是強勢市場的特徵，後續繼續走揚的機率遠大於立刻崩盤。然而，鑑於目前市
場寬度收窄、權重高度集中於科技股的現狀，盲目追高個股的風險已顯著增加。投資建議可以利用ETF掌握大盤趨勢，由於

指數上漲主要由龍頭股驅動，透過市值型ETF(如追蹤標普500ETF)能直接享受龍頭巨頭帶來的指數創高紅利，避免選錯個股
而賺了指數、賠了價差。此外，提防短期股市回檔，可運用定期定額分批布局或等待大盤適度拉回整理再行加碼，降低追高
風險。
創新高時不同水準的市場寬度佔比
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22
已開發市場和新興市場創新高時的寬度

顧
法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓
• 法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓：法國各地抗議活動持續擴大，最初由學生發起，隨後因生活成本高漲而引發其
他群體響應。在財政空間有限、俄烏戰爭與美伊衝突推升能源價格的背景下，政府難以有效緩解民生成本壓力，法國金融市
場因此面臨股債雙跌的局面。
• 公債殖利率攀升，但系統性風險仍相對可控：市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10
年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標（CISS）仍處於
歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐元區主權債務危機期間，顯示金融市場尚未出現廣泛的系統性壓力，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務
危機的風險仍相對有限。
G7社會動盪指數，法國高居榜首

資料來源： Beco models country，2026.10.19 資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.19

資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.15 資料來源： tradingeconomics，2026.10.21
日本貿易收支
• 日本貿易逆差擴大，進口增幅高於出口：日本2026年9月貿易逆差擴大至5,366億日圓，較去年同期的2,777億日圓增加93%，
高於市場預期的1,810億日圓，連續第五個月呈現貿易赤字。出口年增16.7%至10.97兆日圓，受惠於半導體相關電子零件、
製造設備及汽車出口成長；惟進口年增18.9%至11.51兆日圓，增幅高於出口，主要受到原油、半導體電子零件及液化天然
氣進口增加帶動。其中，原油進口年增51.1%，部分反映日本在荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張下，積極分散能源採購來源。
• 日本央行本週料維持利率不變：日本央行9月將政策利率調升25個基點至1.25%，創31年來新高。考量前幾次升息對經濟的

傳導效果仍待觀察，加上貿易逆差擴大反映進口成本壓力，多數分析師預期日本央行本週將暫停升息，以評估貨幣緊縮對經
濟與通膨的影響。

中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期：顯示中國經濟在第二季落底後呈現溫和回升，從季度表現來看，
一、二、三季增速分別為5.0%、4.3%及4.5%，反映政策支持與外需復甦開始發揮效果，全年達成官方4.5%成長目標的機率
明顯提升，第三季經濟改善的主要動能來自出口，受惠於全球企業持續擴建AI基礎設施，及科技產品需求強勁，中國高科技
產品出口表現亮眼，尤其是積體電路、伺服器、電腦零組件，及儲存設備等產品增幅顯著，高技術製造業維持雙位數成長。
同時，中國對美國及歐盟出口維持強勁，推升貿易順差走升，為支撐經濟的重要力量。然而，內需疲弱仍是當前經濟最大隱
憂，房地產調整尚未結束，居民消費與企業投資意願保守，使經濟復甦結構呈現「外熱內冷」的特徵。

• 展望後市：在出口與科技製造業帶動下，短期成長可望維持穩定，但若內需未能有效改善，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散，建
議聚焦在半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈，及高端製造相關產業，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股，仍需觀察後續刺激政
策與消費信心回升情況。
資料來源：開源證券，2026.10.22

彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高
• 彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高：彭博產業研究認為，中國AI產業未來將由大型科技平台主導，其中騰
訊、字節跳動與阿里巴巴憑藉龐大用戶基礎、數據資源、資金實力及完整生態系統，在AI競賽中具備明顯優勢，這些企業可將
AI廣泛應用於電商、社群、遊戲、金融與雲端業務，提升營運效率並強化競爭門檻，例如：阿里巴巴利用 AI 驅動電商平台上的
個人化產品推薦，而騰訊則運用 AI 優化短影音廣告並強化微信功能。騰訊自 2026 年 7 月起開始將混元 AI 模型整合至其電子
遊戲中，使遊戲角色及助理能更準確地回應玩家，然而，AI模型訓練與基礎建設投入龐大，獲利能力短期仍受壓抑，且須面對
監管、資料隱私及晶片供應等挑戰。此外，智譜、月之暗面、MiniMax及商湯等AI新創雖具技術創新能力，但缺乏規模效益、

穩定現金流及長期資本支持，未來獲利挑戰較大。
• 投資建議：看好中國AI長期發展者，可優先關注具資金、算力與應用場景優勢的大型平台企業，受惠程度可望高於多數AI新創
公司。

台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高，台股第四季有望先蹲後跳
• 台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高：9月外銷訂單1,142.1億美元，連兩個月破千億美元，再創單月新高；年增率高達62.4%，連續
20個月呈雙位數正成長。經濟部表示，AI強勁需求超乎預期，預估今年第四季接單還會優於第三季。展望10月表現，外銷
訂單將持續亮麗，估落在1,125億至1,159億美元，有望連三個月破千億美元。
• 台股第四季有望先蹲後跳：10月初以來，台股在盤中觸及歷史高點49,968點後面臨一波修正，主要反映在新一輪的財報公
布前，市場觀望AI投資是否存在泡沫化風險。短期來看，台股在季線附近出現支撐，不過在11月初的美國期中選舉落幕前，
台股維持區間震盪的可能性較高；中期來看，美國期中選後往往因政治不確定性下降，歷史統計股市上漲機率高，加上台灣

年底集團作帳及選舉行情，正向看待年底台股表現。
資料來源：經濟部統計處，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.20。

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9番 1号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー

2026年 11月 18日 全8頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 10月貿易統計 
自動車やAI関連の輸出が堅調も、原油高で貿易赤字は継続

経済調査部 エコノミスト ビリング 安奈

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 10月の貿易統計によると、輸出金額は前年比＋20.5％と 14カ月連続で増加し、
季節調整値でも前月比＋3.3％と 2カ月ぶりに増加した。輸入金額は前年比＋21.6％と
9カ月連続で増加した（季節調整値は前月比＋3.5％）。貿易収支は▲4,765億円、季節
調整値では▲7,672億円と、ともに 6カ月連続の赤字となった。 
◼ 中東情勢を受けた供給制約は続いているものの、代替調達の進展により 10月の原油及
び粗油（以下、原油）の輸入数量は前年同月とおおむね同水準となった。だが、輸入単
価は高止まりしており、中東情勢に起因する供給制約は原油高を通じて輸入金額を押
し上げ、貿易赤字が継続している。 
◼ 輸出数量（季節調整値）は前月比＋4.0％と 3カ月ぶりに増加した。乗用車や半導体等
製造装置、非鉄金属などの増加が全体を押し上げた。地域別では、米国向け、EU向け、
アジア向けのいずれも増加した。アジア向けのうち中国向けも増加した。 
◼ 先行きの輸出数量は、横ばい圏で推移するとみている。AI関連需要などが引き続き下
支えしよう。ただし、リスク要因はAI関連投資の先行きと中東情勢だ。AI関連投資の
持続性には不透明感があり、資本財輸出の下押し要因となり得る。中東からの原油輸
出には回復の動きが見込まれているものの、中東情勢をめぐる不透明感は依然として
残る。

### Page 2

1

2 / 8

【貿易金額】自動車やAI関連の輸出が堅調も、原油高で貿易赤字は継続 
2026年10月の貿易統計によると、輸出金額は前年比＋20.5％とコンセンサス（同＋20.4％、
Bloomberg調査）とほぼ一致し、14カ月連続で増加した（図表 1）。輸出増加の主因は輸出価格
（同＋13.9％）の上昇だ。円安効果があったほか、旺盛な AI・半導体需要を背景に半導体等電
子部品や半導体等製造装置などの価格が上昇していることが寄与したとみられる。輸出の実勢
を示す輸出数量は同＋5.8％と堅調に推移した。世界的な AI需要を背景に半導体関連財の輸出
が堅調だったほか、9月に自動車の挽回生産が進んだこともあり、10月の自動車輸出は比較的
強めの動きになった可能性がある。また、燃料高を背景に燃費性能に優れているハイブリッド
車への需要の高まりも輸出増を後押ししたと考えられる。輸出金額の季節調整値は前月比＋
3.3％と 2カ月ぶりに増加した（図表 2左）。輸出価格（以下、数量と価格の季節調整値は大和
総研による）は低下した一方、輸出数量が上昇し、輸出金額を押し上げた。 
輸入金額は前年比＋21.6％と 9カ月連続で増加した（図表 1）。輸入の実勢を示す輸入数量は
同▲0.2％だった一方、輸入価格が同＋21.9％と大幅に上昇し、金額を押し上げた。輸入価格
の上昇の背景には、中東情勢による原油及び粗油（以下、原油）や液化石油ガスの価格上昇が
ある。特に、代替調達先となっている米国からの原油輸入は数量面（同＋738.2％）と価格面
（同＋384.6％）で上昇傾向が続いており、米国からの輸入金額は同＋1122.8％と高い伸び率と
なった。輸入金額の季節調整値は前月比＋3.5％と増加した（図表2右）。  
10 月の原油の輸入数量は前年比▲0.7％とおおむね前年並みとなったが、輸入価格（同＋
61.2％）が大きく上昇したことで輸入金額（同＋60.5％）が押し上げられた。こうした中東情
勢に起因する供給制約による原油価格の高騰が引き続き輸入金額を押し上げ、貿易赤字が継続
する要因となっている（図表 3）。貿易収支は▲4,765億円、季節調整値では▲7,672億円と、
ともに6カ月連続の赤字となった（図表 4）。

図表 1：貿易統計の概況

（注 1）税関長公示レートは円/ドルレート。コンセンサスは Bloomberg。 
（注 2）数量と価格の季節調整値は大和総研による。 
（出所）財務省、Bloombergより大和総研作成

【輸出数量】主要国・地域向けがそろって増加 
輸出数量（季節調整値）は前月比＋4.0％と3カ月ぶりに増加した。乗用車の増加が全体を押
し上げたほか、グローバルなAI関連需要を背景に半導体等製造装置や非鉄金属も増加に寄与し
た。振れの大きい船舶も下支えした。地域別では、米国向け（同＋7.9％）、EU 向け（同＋
15.7％）、アジア向け（同＋0.5％）のいずれも増加した。アジア向けのうち中国向け（同＋
5.4％）も増加した（図表 5左）。  
米国向けは 3カ月ぶりに増加した。前月に自動車の挽回生産が進んだことや、燃料高により
ハイブリッド車の販売が好調であることから、乗用車は増加した。医薬品は前月の反動で増加
したほか、半導体等製造装置は AI需要の恩恵を受け、4カ月連続で増加した。 
EU向けは4カ月ぶりに増加した。対米輸出と同様に、乗用車の増加が大きく寄与した。EU向
けの乗用車輸出は 3カ月連続で堅調であり、ハイブリッド車への需要の高まりが反映されてい
る。二輪自動車や非鉄金属、原動機なども増加した。 
アジア向けと、そのうち中国向けはともに 2カ月連続で増加した。アジア向けの増加の主因
は、2カ月連続で振れの大きい船舶の増加だった。半導体等製造装置や乗用車、非鉄金属など
も増加した。中国向けも半導体等製造装置や乗用車、非鉄金属などが増加した。

図表 5：地域別の輸出数量（季節調整値、3 カ月後方移動平均・単月）、海外景気と輸出数量

方移動平均、破線は単月の推移を示す。中国向けはアジア向けの内数。

【見通し】輸出数量は横ばい圏で推移の見込み、AI関連需要と中東情勢がリスク要因 
先行きの輸出数量は、横ばい圏で推移するとみている。AI関連需要などが引き続き下支え要
因になろう。ただし、AI関連投資の先行きには不確実性もあり、その持続性には引き続き注意
が必要だ。また、中東からの原油輸出には回復の動きが見込まれているものの、中東情勢をめ
ぐる不透明感は依然として残る。 
11 月に入ってもホルムズ海峡周辺で船舶への攻撃が続いているが1、国際エネルギー機関
（IEA）の11月石油市場リポートによると、米国とイランの軍事衝突後初めて、原油市場は供給
超過に転じる見込みである。中東からの原油輸出が回復しつつあることは好材料であるものの、
同リポートでは下振れリスクが引き続き大きいとも指摘されている2。中東情勢の緊迫化に伴う
資源価格や海上輸送コスト（運賃・保険料）の高止まりが長期化すれば、物流制約に加え、海
外経済の下振れを通じて、輸出数量の下押し要因となり得る。 
米国向けは、AI関連需要を背景とした米国の設備投資の増加が追い風となり、増加すると見
込まれる。ただし、一部のAI関連企業では収益性や財務面への懸念が広がっているほか、米中
間選挙で民主党が上下両院を奪還することが確実となったことで、AIやデータセンターをめぐ
る規制強化への懸念も高まっている。設備投資の拡大が持続するか不透明だ3。設備投資の増勢
が鈍化すれば、日本からの資本財輸出にも下押し圧力がかかる可能性がある。 
アジア向けは横ばい圏で推移するだろう。このうち中国向けはやや軟調な推移が見込まれる。
中国の 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率は前年比＋4.5％となり、2026年の政府成長率目標で
ある 4.5％～5.0％の下限と同水準となった4。中国では内需が引き続き低迷している。景気刺
激策による下支え効果が期待されるものの、需要の弱さが日本からの輸出の逆風になる構図は
続く見込みだ。 
欧州向けは横ばい圏での推移となろう。欧州では、ドイツを中心とした財政拡張を背景に、
防衛支出やインフラ投資が景気を下支えすると見込まれる5。これが欧州経済の回復につながれ
ば、日本からの財輸出も持ち直すことが期待される。ただし、足元では長期金利の上昇が加速
しており、これにより設備投資や公共投資の拡大が抑制される恐れがある。また、エネルギー
価格の高止まりによる個人消費の下振れも重しとなり得る。

1 「中東でタンカー攻撃続く、緊張緩和期待に逆風」（ロイター、2026年11月 10日公開、同 11月 11日更
新） 
2 International Energy Agency (IEA) “Oil Market Report - November 2026”（2026年11月13日） 
3 畑中宏仁・神田慶司「ハイパースケーラーの投資動向が世界経済に与える影響」（大和総研レポート、2026
年 11月 12日） 
4 齋藤尚登「中国経済見通し：不動産不況の底打ちに期待」（大和総研レポート、2026年10月22日） 
5 橋本政彦「7-9月期ユーロ圏GDP 減速しつつも上振れ」（大和総研レポート、2026年11月2日）

### Page 1

 Update
Silergy Corp.  | Asia Pacific
  3Q26 Margin miss from higher 
production cost and opex; stay 
UW 
3Q26 results miss from incremental production cost and higher opex: Silergy’s 
3Q26 GM of 47.4% and OPM of 13.3% missed MSe and consensus despite a better 
product mix (faster growth in AI and automotive). We attribute the miss to rising 
foundry/OSAT costs and higher AI datacenter R&D. Management guided opex to 
stay roughly flat in 1H27 and that it could be higher in 2H27 with new hires.
AI-related revenue and Gen4 process are key drivers for 2027 margin recovery: 
Management said 2027 revenue could grow at least 20-30% YoY, with AI-related 
revenue at c.25% and Gen4 at 50% of total revenue by end-2027. Management also 
guided 2027 GM to be >50% while continuing passing on cost to customers.
Automotive revenue could account for 20% of 2027 total revenue: Management 
expects strong automotive growth in 2027, with dollar content per high-end vehicle 
rising from US$220 in 2026 to US$270-300 in 2027.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Silergy Corp. (6415.TW)
Base case, residual income model. We assume a cost of equity of 9.5% (2.0% risk-free rate, 6%
risk premium, beta of 1.25), an intermediate growth rate of 13.5%, and a terminal growth rate
of 4.5%.
Risks to Upside
n Gross margin expansion amid stabilized competition
n A surge in LED demand as pricing drops to the sweet spot
n Greater-than-expected benefits from China's support in local semiconductors

n Higher-than-expected dividend payout ratio
Risks to Downside
n Lower gross margin amid intensified competition
n Weaker-than-expected benefits from China's support in local semiconductors
n Lower-than-expected dividend payout ratio

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

## Document 165: 261119_ubs_gudeng

Source PDF: all report pdf/261119_ubs_gudeng.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-19
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

ab 19 November 2026
Global Research
Gudeng Precision Industrial
Broad-based 3Q beat sets up a solid 2027
Solid Q3 earnings beat; momentum continuing into Q4 and 2027
Gudeng’s Q326 results came in above consensus, with revenue 5% and EPS 16% above
VA consensus. The beat was broad-based, driven by stronger revenue, gross margin, and
opex leverage. We expect momentum to continue into Q426, with full-year 2026
revenue growing 34%, vs our prior estimate of 29%, driven by continued strength in

EUV and China FOUP. Q4 gross margin should sustain at 46% level, with better margin
of EUV mask pod. Looking into 2027, we estimate revenue growth of 31%, primarily
volume-driven, with upside if Gudeng can add more capacity for FOUP. Of note,
advanced packaging revenue could potentially reach NT$0.85bn, up meaningfully from
~NT$0.3bn in 2026. Below the line, we estimate FY27 gross margin of 46%, with
favorable mix from EUV and China FOUP, while the opex ratio should improve to 24%

from 25% in 2026.
EUV mask pod sales to grow 30% in 2027
EUV demand has remained consistently strong throughout 2026 and the sales should
grow 43% this year and 30% in 2027. Key drivers are the meaningful leading edge
expansion at TSMC, and Gudeng’s share gain in Intel. We estimate Gudeng’s EUV mask
pod shipment to Intel to likely close to triple YoY this year. DUV mask pod sales should
also grow 15-20% in 2027, supported by stable demand from China logic customers.

Meaningful recovery in China FOUP lifting ASP and gross margin
On top of the EUV strength, the business recovery in China FOUP is equally important in
re-accelerating Gudeng’s earnings and margin improvement. Gudeng has seen strong
demand from more diversified client base across logic and memory YTD, versus in the
past when growth was more reliant on tier 1 logic clients like SMIC. Considering the
tight supply at global peers, we anticipate the growth of China FOUP to sustain and

support 40% foup revenue growth in 2027. On TSMC side, we believe Gudeng should
also be improving product mix through 2027 with the upgraded diffuser wafer foup
with higher ASP and margin for N2. On advanced packaging, Gudeng is set to ramp up
sales more meaningfully from NT$300mn this year to NT$0.85bn in 2027, mostly
through large panel FOUP for substrate makers, but also good volume for CoWoS
related FOUP.
Valuation: Maintain Buy with lower price target of NT$610 from NT$710

Post Q3 results, we upgraded 2026/27 EPS by 14% and 2%. We lower our price target
from NT$710 to NT$610 based on a lower multiple of 26x 27-28E PE from 30x
previously, considering the broader sector de-rating and lower LT earnings CAGR of
26% from 29%. Maintain Buy.

Forecast returns

Company Description
Gudeng Precision Industrial was founded in 1998 and entered semiconductor front-end
process supply chain in 2000. It manufactures reticle and wafer carriers,which provide
protection, delivery and storage solutions; it also supplies related equipment. According to

the company, it is the largest supplier of reticle carriers with a 70% market share globally and
holds an 85% market share for EUV POD, while its FOUP is adopted by over 50% of
customers in Greater China as the baseline.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Gudeng based on PE methodology.
Downside risk for the semis industry includes: 1) slower-than-expected N2 migration, 2)
geopolitical uncertainties that weigh on the semis cycle, and 3) meaningful deceleration of

new technology deveopment like Cloud AI, autonomous driving etc.
Downside risk for the company itself includes: 1) slower-than-expected share expansion in
FOUP market, 2) executions on pushing new products like diffuser FOUP, high NA EUV pods.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

奇鋐 10 月營收 221.03 億元(MoM + 5.16%，YoY + 34.35%)，展
望 4Q26，受惠 NVIDIA Vera Rubin、AWS Trainium3 及 Google
新一代 TPU 等 GPU 與 ASIC 平台持續拉貨，液冷產品出貨放量，
加上高 ASP 產品占比提升，預期營收及毛利率可望持續季增。展
望 2027 年，隨 CSP 持續擴大自研 ASIC 投資，預期 ASIC 營收貢
獻將超越 GPU，且主要 GPU 及 ASIC 運算晶片水冷滲透率將持續
提升。配合垂直供電(VPD)架構導入，雙面水冷板需求將明顯增
加，預期 2027 年至少過半主要客戶採用，帶動冷板用量及單機散
熱產值提升。產能方面，目前水冷月產能約 100 萬件，規劃 2027
年擴增逾 50%，並以越南為主要擴產基地，同步擴充機殼及 CDU
產能。此外，CDU 已開始出貨中國客戶，歐美客戶持續送樣驗證，
並積極布局雙相液冷及新型高導熱材料。太空散熱方面，公司已與
新舊客戶展開合作，目前仍處於 NPI 及樣品測試階段，預期產品
ASP 將優於現有產品，長期有望成為新成長動能。綜合以上，研
究部預估奇鋐 2026 年營業收入為 2,217.44 億元(YoY +58.80%)，
毛利率為 32.60%(YoY +6.79ppt)，營業利益率為 27.34%(YoY 
+7.61ppt)，稅後純益為 414.40 億元(YoY +115.99%)，EPS 為
105.07 元；2027 年營業收入為 3,402.77 億元(YoY +53.45%)，毛
利率為 34.41%(YoY +1.81ppt) ，營業利益率為 29.38%(YoY 
+2.04ppt)，稅後純益為 676.24 億元(YoY +63.18%)，EPS 為
171.46 元。公司近三年歷史本益比區間 10~25 倍，考量 ASIC 需
求擴張及水冷滲透率持續提升，帶動單機散熱產值成長，研究部以
2027 年 EPS 給予 25 倍本益比，目標價 4285 元，投資建議為買
進。

⚫ 公司基本資料 奇鋐(3017.TT)公司簡介 
奇鋐科技股份有限公司(3017.TT)成立於 1991 年，2002 年掛牌上市，為
國內營收規模最大的散熱模組製造商。公司產品涵蓋風扇、散熱模組、
石墨片、熱管、均熱板、水冷板、機殼與機櫃，亦具備整合組裝、觸控
模組及相機模組能力，提供完整散熱解決方案與系統整合服務。公司業
務應用涵蓋桌上型電腦、筆記型電腦、伺服器、通訊設備(基地台、Switch)
與手機等多元終端，並自 2019 年起大舉進軍伺服器市場，陸續取得中系
股本(億) 39.26

單季歷史新高，主要受惠 AI 伺服器液冷需求強勁、高 ASP 產品出貨增
加及產品組合優化。毛利率季增主要受惠高附加價值水冷產品占比提升
及自動化改善生產效率。產品組合方面，3Q26 散熱產品營收占比約
68.5%，機殼約 17.1%，其中水冷產品持續放量，為主要成長動能。綜

液冷滲透率持續提升，2027 年營運成長動能強勁
奇鋐 10 月營收 221.03 億元(MoM + 5.16%，YoY + 34.35%)，展望
4Q26，受惠 NVIDIA Vera Rubin、AWS Trainium3 及 Google 新一代 TPU
等 GPU 與 ASIC 平台持續拉貨，液冷產品出貨放量，加上高 ASP 產品
占比提升，預期營收及毛利率可望持續季增。展望 2027 年，隨 CSP 持
續擴大自研 ASIC 投資，預期 ASIC 營收貢獻將超越 GPU，且主要 GPU
及 ASIC 運算晶片水冷滲透率將持續提升。配合垂直供電(VPD)架構導
入，雙面水冷板需求將明顯增加，預期 2027 年至少過半主要客戶採用，
帶動冷板用量及單機散熱產值提升。產能方面，目前水冷月產能約 100

萬件，規劃 2027 年擴增逾 50%，並以越南為主要擴產基地，同步擴充
機殼及 CDU 產能。此外，CDU 已開始出貨中國客戶，歐美客戶持續送
樣驗證，並積極布局雙相液冷及新型高導熱材料。太空散熱方面，公司
已與新舊客戶展開合作，目前仍處於 NPI 及樣品測試階段，預期產品 ASP
將優於現有產品，長期有望成為新成長動能。綜合以上，研究部預估奇
鋐 2026 年營業收入為 2,217.44 億元(YoY +58.80%)，毛利率為
32.60%(YoY +6.79ppt)，營業利益率為 27.34%(YoY +7.61ppt)，稅後純
益為 414.40 億元(YoY +115.99%)，EPS 為 105.07 元；2027 年營業收
入為 3,402.77 億元(YoY +53.45%)，毛利率為 34.41%(YoY +1.81ppt)，
營業利益率為 29.38%(YoY +2.04ppt)，稅後純益為 676.24 億元(YoY
+63.18%)，EPS 為 171.46 元。

上品為從事氟素樹脂材料相關製品之生產
廠商，主要經營氟素樹脂材料（鐵氟龍）相
關製品，包括氟素原料加工之半成品及成品
生產及銷售，下游應用廣泛，是全球氟素樹
脂加工應用廠商中少數能同時滿足各階段
設備、製造、研發、創新、設計等完整服務
的企業。3Q26 產品營收比重，氟素內襯設
備營收占 69.7% ， 氟 素 內 襯 管 件 類 占
10.2%，氟素應用材料類占 16.5 %，氟素

別區分，台灣占 47.4%，中國占 48.2%，美

本次報告：法說會

上品 (4770 TT) 
在手訂單金額提升，持續擴充產能因應

永豐觀點 
上品在手訂單金額提升至 80 億元，隨著先前低毛利訂單陸續消化完畢，產品組合
預期改善，公司規劃於 2027 持續進行產能擴充，後市展望持續正向，研究部維持
正向看待上品獲利表現。 
 投資評價與建議 
投資建議維持買進，主要係因：上品在手訂單金額持續提升至 80 億元，反映台灣
及中國地區接單表現持續暢旺，隨著公司低毛利訂單出貨完畢，產品組合應逐步好
轉，且公司規劃於 2027 持續擴充產能，挹注未來營收動能，研究部預估公司
2026/2027 稅後 EPS 14.55/23.01 元，參考歷史 PER 區間約位於 10-20X，以
2027 年預估 EPS，給予 15X PER 評價，目標價 345 元，投資建議維持買進。

ESG 評析 
上品企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B+等與 B+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。

### Page 2

2

上品為從事氟素樹脂材料相關製品之生產廠商：公司成立於 1981 年，主要經營氟
素樹脂材料（鐵氟龍）相關製品，包括氟素原料加工之半成品及成品生產及銷售，
下游應用廣泛，是全球氟素樹脂加工應用廠商中少數能同時滿足各階段設備、製
造、研發、創新、設計等完整服務的企業。3Q26 產品營收比重，氟素內襯設備營
收占 69.7%，氟素內襯管件類占 10.2%，氟素應用材料類占 16.5 %，氟素工程收
入占 3.7%；3Q26 公司產品依應用類別區分，半導體占營收 95.1%，化工應用占
比為 3.1%，面板及太陽能應用占 1.4%，其他應用占 0.4%，3Q26 公司營收按地
區別區分，台灣占 47.4%，中國占 48.2%，美國占 0.5%，其他亞洲占 1.6%，歐
洲占 2.2%。 
3Q26 EPS 4.27 元，優於預期：上品 3Q26 營收 16 億元（+22.5% QoQ，+14.4% 
YoY），優於預期，主因上品於 2Q26 擴產，並持續拉升產能利用率，推升營收表
現，毛利率 34.64%（-1.91pts. QoQ，+4.91pts. YoY），低於預期，主因上品出
貨較多低毛利訂單，影響產品組合所致，稅後淨利 34.1 億元（+37.2% QoQ，
+39.9% YoY），優於預期，其中，營業費用低於預期，主因認列預期信用損失金
額降低所致，3Q26 稅後 EPS 4.27 元，優於預期。

在手訂單金額持續提升：上品在手訂單金額達 80 億元，較前次發報告時 75 億元
持續提升，其中，約 75%為彰濱廠訂單，約 25%為嘉興廠訂單，預期到年底在手
訂單金額將持續再提升。2026 接單槽車數量達 1,100 台，反映台灣化學品廠因應
晶圓廠大客戶需求而擴充產能，對於上品內襯設備及內襯管件需求同步增溫，而中
國記憶體廠及化學品廠亦持續擴充，下單動能有所增溫。隨著市況於 2026 大幅改
善，彰濱廠及嘉興廠已可挑選毛利率較佳的訂單進行承接，產品組合預期將逐步好
轉，有利其毛利率逐步回升。 
2027 上品計畫持續擴充產能：因應客戶需求強勁，公司於 2Q26 進行擴產，並持
續招工補充人力，拉升產能利用率，目前彰濱廠及嘉興廠已達近滿載水準，公司年
初彰濱廠槽車約當產能約為月產能 80 台，近期已提升至月產能 130 台，預期年底
將達滿載規模月產能 140 台，公司規劃將於 6M27 再擴充至月產能 180 台，嘉興
廠亦規劃提升產能約 30%，以因應客戶強勁需求。而先前承接的低毛利訂單仍有
部分尚未出貨完畢，預期將於 1Q27 出貨完畢，因營收比重持續下降，預期產品組
合應持續改善，有利毛利率及獲利表現回升。

## Document 209: 6223旺矽 群益

Source PDF: all report pdf/6223旺矽 群益.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 11 月 23 日

1 群益投顧 
旺矽(6223 TT)AI 晶片測試 2027 續旺。旺矽估 2027 年營收成長 50%以上，毛利率維持 60%以上；
2028 年 CPO 設備有望量產，將帶動評價提升，給 Trading Buy 的投資建議。

研 究 員 ： 莊 禮 誠   
dan.chuang@cim.capital.com.tw

旺矽 3Q26 毛 利 率 季 增 ：  
旺矽 3Q26 毛 利 率 達 61.7％ ， 較 2Q26 的 58.4％提升 3.3 個 pts， 單 季 稅 後 純
益 18.80 億 元 ， 季 增 23％ 、 年 增 114％ ， 每 股 稅 後 純 益 19.18 元 ， 顯 示 高 階
產 品 需 求 放 量 ， 帶 動 獲 利 能 力 顯 著 改 善 。 旺 矽 3Q26 毛 利 率 突 破 60%， 主 要
受惠 AI 及 HPC 相 關 產 品 出 貨 比 重 提 高 。  
旺 矽 估 4Q26 營 收 季 增 ：  
AI 及 高 效 能 運 算 (HPC)晶 片 測 試 需 求 持 續 升 溫，帶 動 測 試 介 面 大 廠 旺 矽 營 運
動 能 走 強。隨 先 進 製 程 晶 片 複 雜 度 提 高、測 試 接 點 數 持 續 增 加，旺 矽 高 階 探
針 卡 需 求 維 持 強 勁，旺矽估 4Q26 營 收 可 望 季 增 5％至 10％，4Q26、2027 全
年 毛 利 率 有 機 會 維 持 60%以 上 水 準 ， 營 運 成 長 動 能 延 續 至 2027 年。 
旺 矽 估 4Q26 毛 利 率 與 3Q26 相當：  
隨 AI 晶 片 持 朝 更 高 運 算 效 能 、 更 大 晶 片 面 積 及 先 進 封 裝 架 構 發 展 ， 測 試 接
點 數 同 步 攀 升，對 探 針 卡 精 密 度、可 靠 度 及 測 試 效 率 的 要 求 也 愈 來 愈 高。旺
矽 憑 藉 高 階 測 試 技 術 及 客 戶 認 證 優 勢 ， 有 利 擴 大 先 進 晶 片 測 試 市 場 布 局 。  
值得注意的是，隨高階探針卡技術門檻提高，相關產品製造成本也同步增
加，旺 矽 具 備 將 部 分 成 本 轉 嫁 客 戶 的 能 力，有 助 降 低 成 本 上 升 對 毛 利 率 的 衝
擊 。 加 上 高 附 加 價 值 產 品 出 貨 增 加 ， 4Q26 毛 利 率 仍 有 機 會 維 持 在 60%出頭
水準。 
CPO 設 備 有 望 於 2027 年 拿 到 訂 單 ， 2028 年 貢 獻 顯著營收：  
在 新 技 術 布 局 方 面 ， 共 同 封 裝 光 學 (CPO) 被 視 為 旺 矽 下 一 階 段 重 要 成 長 機
會。隨 AI 資 料 中 心 對 高 速 傳 輸 及 低 功 耗 需 求 提 升，光 電 整 合 技 術 逐 步 導 入，
將 進 一 步 提 高 相 關 晶 片 與 模 組 測 試 複 雜 度，為 測 試 介 面 供 應 商 創 造 新 商 機。 
目前旺矽 CPO 相 關 測 試 方 案 持 續 推 進 客 戶 驗 證 ， 其 中 第 三 階 段 (Insertion 3)
已 向 多 家 客 戶 交 付 工 程 測 試 設 備 ， 預 期 1H27 有 機 會 陸 續 取 得 量 產 設 備 訂
單；第 二 階 段 (Insertion 2) 預計 12/2026 取 得 首 家 客 戶 認 證 結 果，後 續 仍 須 視
客 戶 驗 證 及 導 入 進 度 而 定 。  
旺 矽 持 續 擴 充 探 針 卡 產 能 ：  
此 外 ， 旺 矽 2027 年 擴 產 規 劃 也 是 市 場 關 注 焦 點 。 隨 AI 及 HPC 需 求 延 續 ，
高階探針卡產能配置將攸關後續訂單承接能力，公司預期在年度預算規劃
完成後，2026 年 底 前 可 望 進 一 步 釐 清 2027 年 產 能 擴 充 方 向 。  
投 資 建 議 ：  
旺矽估 2027 年 營 收 成 長 50%以 上 ， 毛 利 率 維 持 60%以 上 ； 2028 年 CPO 設
備 有 望 量 產 ， 將 帶 動 評 價 提 升 ， 給 Trading Buy 的 投 資 建 議 。

### Page 3

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 11 月 23 日

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】  
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 ≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <15%

中立(Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等  
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整  
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】  
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較 3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為 四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等 係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。   
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」 及 「 區 間 操 作 」 均有 upside 目 標 價 。 差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。  
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ：  
1. 達 目 標 價 。  
2. 雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。  
3. 推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】 
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 247: [012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick_cd366e43

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick_cd366e43.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(012450) 
한화에어로스페이스 
2026.10.30

AI 로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 1,660,000 원(유지) 
 현재주가 760,000 원(10/29)

 3Q26 연결 매출액은 6조 943억원(-6.0%yoy), 영업이익은 4,520억원(-
46.2%yoy, OPM 7.4%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센 1조 1,614억원을 하회.  
 지상방산의 손익이 크게 악화. 영업이익은 217억원(OPM 1.7%) 기록. 주요 
납품이 4분기에 집중된 가운데 일회성 비용 약 500억원(대전사업장 복구·
정상화 약 300억원, 국내 사업 충당금 약 160억원)과 환율 영향이 겹침.   
 다만 내용을 살펴보면 수출 사업의 수익성 훼손은 크지 않았다는 판단임.  
 일회성 제외 영업이익은 약 717억원(OPM 5.8%)이며, 일회성 비용은 대전
사업장이 국내 물량을 전담한다는 점에서 전액 국내 귀속으로 추정. 내수 
OPM을 일회성 제외 -5% 내외로 가정하면, 내수 영업이익은 약 -826억원
(일회성 포함 OPM -12.7%), 수출은 1,043억원(OPM 17.6%)으로 추정됨. 
 1Q26 대비로는 국내 마진 하락이, 2Q26 대비로는 수출 물량 감소가 가장 
크게 작용(도표 참고). 국내는 저마진 사업 매출이 늘고 고마진 반복 양산 물
량이 줄며 일시적으로 믹스가 악화. 수출은 폴란드 등 주요 인도가 정체되어 
매출이 감소한 가운데 저수익 사업 중심으로 믹스가 악화된 영향. 
 4Q26부터 회복 구간 진입 전망. 폴란드 K9 2차 실행계약 물량의 현지 인
도가 본격화되며, 회사는 2026년 폴란드 K9 30문 이상, 천무 발사대 60대 
이상(*연초 40대에서 상향) 인도를 계획. 수출입 데이터상으로도 폴란드·루
마니아향 출하가 대규모로 진행 중이며 폴란드향 부속류 수출도 정상적으로 
이어지는 것으로 확인. 일회성 소멸과 고마진 수출 집중으로 수출 OPM은 
30% 내외로 회복할 것. 다만 국내는 정상 가동(2027년 상반기 목표) 전까
지 한 자릿수 초반 마진에 머물 것으로 전망. 
 중장기 수익성 우려도 크지 않다는 판단. 2027~2029년 매출 연 15% 성
장 가이던스는 유지됐고, 내년 폴란드 K9·천무 인도 물량은 모두 올해 대비 
증가할 계획. 폴란드 사업 이후 높아진 수출 마진 레벨은 후속 사업에서도 
유지될 전망. 수주는 중동 지정학 불안으로 지연되고 있으나 대공체계 등 문
의가 이어지고 있어, 2027년부터 중동이 수주의 중심이 될 것으로 기대. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 166만원을 유지함. 3Q26 지상방산 
쇼크는 국내 적자와 수출 물량 공백이 겹친 일시적 결과로, 수출 경쟁력 훼
손으로 보기 어려움. 실적 충격에 따른 주가 조정은 분할 매수 기회로 판단.  
 한화에어로스페이스의 주가는 4월 7일 사상 최고가 153.7만원에서 10월 
29일 76.0만원으로 -51% 조정 받은 상황. 12MF P/E 기준 14.3x, 27E 
P/E는 13.9x로 밸류에이션 관점에서도 매력적인 구간 진입. 그동안 3분기 
실적 우려 등으로 부진했다면, 이제는 실적 바텀아웃과 대형 수주 랠리 속 
다시 한 번 반등을 기록할 차례. 동사를 방산업종 Top-pick으로 유지함.

시가총액(십억원) 39,188

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

## Document 260: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발_5484f7f5

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발_5484f7f5.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적 
2026-10-29

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 640,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 285,500 원 
상승여력: 124.2%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 감소 및 환율 하락으로 시장 예상치 하회 
Key: 4Q26~1Q27 부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 수주 모멘텀 전망 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 엔진&FDC 으로 밸류 매력도 충분. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 시장예상치 하회하는 실적 시현  
3Q26 연결 매출액 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영업이익 8,992억원
(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(9,792억원)를 하회했다. 조업일수 감소
(휴가 10 일+중대재해 4일) 및 환율 하락 영향(450 억원)과 더불어, 임단협 관련 예상 비
용 중 75%를 이번 분기 선반영 했기 때문이다. 상선 수주 빈티지는 중형 선박 기준 ‘23 년 
9.6%, ‘24년 65%, ‘25년 25%, 대형 선박 기준 ‘22년 4%, ‘23년 45%, ‘24년 31%, 
‘25년 20%로, 선가 상승효과 지속되는 중이다. 특히 해양 부문에서 루야 프로젝트의 공정
률 확대 효과 및 C/O로 180억원의 환입 발생으로 전 분기 대비 큰 폭의 이익 성장 시현했
다. 최근 키윗과 미국 내 합작 조선소 설립을 추진하며, 미 군수지원함 초도 2척 수주 및 향
후 물량 건조, 그리고 HII와의 함정 선체블록 하도급 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 64 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 82 만원에서 64 만원
으로 하향한다. 최근 상선 실적 피크아웃 우려에 따른 조선주 주가 급락으로 멀티플 하향 조
정했다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 
FDC 에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지속되고 있는 중이다. 올해 공시 기
준으로 육상발전 엔진 약 1.6GW 수주한 것으로 알려졌으나, 미공시된 약 1.4GW 까지 포
함 시 총 3.0GW 이상을 수주한 상황이다. 캐파 증설 기준으로 ‘28 년 1.5GW, ‘29 년 
3GW 캐파에 해당하는 물량을 이미 대부분 채웠으며, 기존 계획 대비 빠른 준공 혹은 공정
률 확대, 더 나아가 추가적인 캐파 증설까지 고려할 수준으로 파악된다. 빠르면 올해 연말 
혹은 내년 초부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 본격 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 
전망이다. 현 주가 레벨에서 상승여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 3Q 실적 및 임단협 불
확성 해소된 현 시점에서 적극적 매수 의견 및 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)로 제시한다.

## Document 286: Asia Outlook 2027_ Growth broadens, capital gets selective

Source PDF: all report pdf/Asia Outlook 2027_ Growth broadens, capital gets selective.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 25
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

After a stronger-than-expected 2026, Asian growth should
broaden in 2027 as AI tailwinds moderate, lower energy
prices support demand and supply-chain diversiﬁcation
continues. Capital ﬂows will become more selective as global
ﬁnancial conditions tighten. We favor the KRW, TWD, SGD, and
expect Australia and Philippines to lead easing moves
Deepali Bhargava
How did Asia’s economy perform in 2026,
and what will look different in 2027?
2026 was stronger than expected

Asia surprised on the upside in 2026, with regional GDP growth
outperforming expectations, led by strong exports and investment
activity. Investment remained robust across much of the region,
supported by technology-related spending and government-led
infrastructure projects. This enabled export-oriented economies
such as South Korea, Taiwan and Singapore to signiﬁcantly exceed
consensus growth forecasts.

### Page 2

The picture was less favourable in economies more reliant on
domestic demand. Indonesia and the Philippines disappointed
expectations as household consumption remained subdued.
Higher energy prices weighed on real incomes, while the Philippines
also faced a drag from slower government spending amid political
and corruption-related controversies. Both economies attracted
relatively less tech-related foreign direct investment than the
region's manufacturing and semiconductor hubs, limiting support

from global dynamics.
India was a notable outperformer. Despite beneﬁting less directly
from the AI-driven boom and facing higher energy import costs, it
expanded by a robust 8.2% year-on-year in the ﬁrst half of 2026.
Growth continued to be supported by structural reforms, public
capital expenditure and subsidies that cushioned households from
higher fuel prices.
AI-led divergences should narrow in 2027, favouring South Asia
Overall, we expect GDP growth excluding China to slow to 3.8% in

2027, down from 4.5% in 2026. Tight chip supply should continue
to support investment growth through 2027, but as capex growth
slows from today's exceptional pace, AI's contribution to growth is
likely to moderate.
As AI tailwinds fade and the full eﬀects of monetary tightening are
felt, growth in developed Asia, particularly Korea and Australia,
could slow more than in developing Asia. Korea is the clearest
example, where growth has been concentrated in technology-

related sectors while domestic demand, non-tech industries and
broader wage growth have lagged. As in previous investment-led
booms, the beneﬁts have been slower to ﬁlter through to the wider
economy.
By contrast, the Philippines’ 2026 growth disappointment was
driven primarily by policy and political uncertainty, both of which
are likely to prove temporary. India continues to occupy a relative
sweet spot. While it beneﬁted less from the AI boom, it’s also less

exposed to any correction. Structural reforms, infrastructure

### Page 3

spending, and resilient services exports should leave it relatively
well placed as the AI cycle normalises.
The divergence is less pronounced in parts of Southeast Asia,
particularly Singapore and Malaysia, where domestic demand has
remained relatively resilient alongside a strong external sector.
Trade growth likely to moderate in 2027
Asian economies are expected to be the largest contributors to
global trade volume growth for the third consecutive year in 2026.

According to World Trade Organisation forecasts, global trade
growth is expected to moderate in 2027 as AI-related demand
cools from exceptionally strong levels, though the deceleration
should be gradual rather than abrupt.
Recent data has been mixed. Some signs of moderation appear in
semiconductor export growth in Japan and Taiwan, and export
orders softened across the board in October. However,
semiconductor exports from Korea and China continue to post

strong gains. Data from Taiwan suggest it’s still too early to call a
turning point.
Even so, trade growth should remain relatively resilient in 2027,
supported by ongoing supply-chain diversiﬁcation, continued
investment ﬂows and still-elevated levels of AI-related activity. This
is even as the pace of expansion normalises.
Pockets of domestic demand strength should support growth
The structural backdrop for consumption in Japan is becoming
more supportive. Wage growth has increased signiﬁcantly since the

start of the year. Also, households are gradually shifting away from
the cash-heavy behaviour associated with the deﬂation era
towards greater participation in ﬁnancial markets. This supports
wealth creation and consumption.
Singapore should continue to see resilient consumption growth,
supported by strong FDI inﬂows and spillovers from the AI
investment cycle. These tailwinds are feeding into ﬁrm wage
growth and stronger household purchasing power. Singapore has

### Page 4

also experienced one of the largest fuel price increases in the region.
If energy prices correct as expected, lower fuel costs should further
boost consumer spending.
In India, consumer spending was relatively insulated from the
energy price shock, aided by government subsidies and only partial
pass-through of higher input costs. While consumption growth
may moderate in 2027 due to weaker agricultural output from El
Niño and tighter monetary policy, strong government spending

should continue to support rural incomes. Lower input costs as oil
prices correct should help sustain household spending.
Asia GDP growth likely to moderate in 2027
as AI tailwinds fade
Source: CEIC, ING
How is AI reshaping Asia's growth story,
and can the region continue to beneﬁt as
the boom matures?
Looking ahead to 2027, North Asia should continue to dominate AI
trade. Southeast Asia should play an increasingly important role as
its AI story is driven by production, investment and supply-chain

integration rather than AI adoption itself. While the region doesn’t
have the largest AI companies, it’s become indispensable to
hardware and manufacturing ecosystems that make AI possible.
As ﬁrms diversify supply chains and invest in AI infrastructure,
Southeast Asia stands out as one of the largest structural
beneﬁciaries of the AI boom outside North Asia.
Why we remain constructive:

### Page 5

Trade dominance: Asia accounted for about 77% of AI-driven
exports in 2025
Asia sits at the centre of the global AI hardware ecosystem. It
dominates global trade in AI-enabling goods. East Asia, excluding
China, accounts for more than 40% of global exports of AI-related
products. Southeast Asia contributes another quarter despite
having a much smaller share of overall world trade. With China
contributing another 13%, Asia is generating much of the world's

growth in AI-related exports.
The same dynamic is visible on the import side. AI-enabling goods
account for a far larger share of imports than East Asia’s and
Southeast Asia’s share of overall trade, underscoring the deep
integration of global AI supply chains.
Growth in the ﬁrst half of 2026 reinforced Asia's leadership, with the
region's largest AI exporters recording the fastest growth. In the
second quarter, exports of AI-enabling goods surged by 80% YoY.

East Asia’s contribution to global export growth doubled to 32.3
percentage points in just six months, while Southeast Asia’s share
similarly surged to 22.0 percentage points. China alone accounted
for a further 12.8 percentage points, underscoring the region’s
outsized role in driving global trade.
These trade relationships have shifted markedly over the last
decade. One notable change is Southeast Asia's growing share of
US imports, consistent with the rapid build-out of data centres by

hyperscaler ﬁrms. Yet Southeast Asia has lagged in strengthening
trade linkages with East Asia, excluding China, and within the
region itself. This suggests there’s scope for deeper regional
integration as the AI ecosystem expands.
Broadening beneﬁciaries: Asia’s AI story extends beyond
Korea/Taiwan
On products, much of the focus has been on memory chip exports
from Korea and Taiwan. But the beneﬁciaries of the AI boom are
increasingly broadening. ASEAN economies have attracted

substantial foreign direct investment in semiconductors ucand

### Page 6

increased their share of global non-memory chip production.
Singapore and Malaysia now account for more than 33% of global
ampliﬁer chip exports, up from 23% a decade ago. The Philippines
and Thailand have made impressive gains, together accounting for
around 2.5% of global exports.
Looking across the AI value chain, almost all AI-enabling goods
trade is concentrated in intermediate inputs (62%) and equipment
(37%). While East Asia, ex-China, remains the dominant supplier at

every stage of the value chain, Southeast Asia has emerged as a
critical second hub. It accounts for roughly a quarter of global
exports of AI-related equipment, spanning the full value chain from
semiconductor manufacturing to the servers and other
infrastructure deployed in data centres. This is particularly
signiﬁcant because the fastest-growing areas of AI investment are
in physical infrastructure, including data centres, servers, and
semiconductor packaging and testing, where Southeast Asia has

built a strong competitive advantage.
Southeast Asia's importance is especially evident in the middle of
the value chain. Together, East Asia and Southeast Asia account for
nearly 90% of global trade in AI-related intermediate inputs, with
Southeast Asia contributing around 23%.
While growth in AI-related demand may eventually moderate,
Asia's central role in hardware and manufacturing ecosystems
should help sustain investment, trade and industrial activity. This

should keep the region a key beneﬁciary of the AI cycle for years to
come.

Southeast Asia gaining ground

### Page 7

Note: AI enabling goods refer to products that facilitate the use of AI in
various sectors, and are part of a list used in 2025 World Trade Report.
Source: WTO
Is AI driving inﬂation or productivity in
Asia? And is it disrupting jobs or
transforming them?
AI's economic impact in Asia is likely to be uneven over time. Signs
of AI-related inﬂation are already visible, particularly in Korea,
where technology-related price increases are contributing
meaningfully to headline CPI. By contrast, evidence of productivity

improvements remains concentrated in parts of the technology
sector and highly AI-complementary occupations. While the
productivity beneﬁts could ultimately prove more signiﬁcant,
they’re likely to emerge gradually and take longer to be reﬂected in
economy-wide data.
AI is starting to show up in Asia's inﬂation data, especially Korea
AI is likely to be inﬂationary in the short term. This is reﬂected in the
sharp rise in memory chip prices and strong tech exports, as

demand for chips continues to outpace supply. Recent consumer
price data suggest electronics prices are beginning to recover
across parts of Asia, with the strongest increases seen in mobile
phones, computers and communication equipment.
The sharpest increases have been in Korea, where data storage
device prices rose around 40% YoY year-to-date. Inﬂation is
accelerating rapidly in these categories. Mobile phone prices rose
11% YoY in October, up from just 2% at the start of the year.

Computer-related prices accelerated to 24% YoY from 6% earlier in

### Page 8

the year. Mobile handset and communication device inﬂation has
also picked up in India and Indonesia, reaching 6-8% YoY.
Contribution to headline inﬂation varies across Asia, but generally
remains low given the lower weights of technology-related
components. Korea is seeing one of the region's largest increases,
with AI-related categories adding about 20bp to headline inﬂation.
In most other Asian economies, the contribution remains below 10
basis points despite price increases, reﬂecting much smaller CPI

weights.
Evidence of AI-related inﬂation in services is less clear-cut.
Information and communication services inﬂation has picked up in
Korea and Malaysia, while falling communication charges continue
to weigh on technology-related CPI components in Singapore.
Overall, however, the contribution to headline inﬂation remains
modest.
Emerging productivity gains
On productivity, the distinction between AI producers and AI users
matters. The IMF ﬁnds that advanced Asian economies like

Singapore, Korea and Japan have a relatively high concentration of
jobs where AI is more likely to augment workers than replace them,
creating greater potential for productivity gains. Around 40% of
jobs in Singapore are estimated to be highly complementary to AI.
The key question, however, is whether these gains are already
visible in the data. Direct evidence remains limited given the short
time frame of AI adoption. Australia is one of the few economies in

the region where available data draw some tentative conclusions.
Our analysis of changes in real output per worker suggests
productivity growth has improved in parts of the technology sector,
particularly telecommunications services. While it’s still too early to
attribute this entirely to AI, the evidence is consistent with
technology adoption beginning to support productivity growth in
selected industries.
Early evidence points to adaptation, not job losses

### Page 9

Evidence from Korea suggests AI may be reshaping labour demand
unevenly. Youth employment has weakened in highly AI-exposed
industries, while employment among workers in their 50s has
increased. India and the Philippines are among the countries most
exposed to AI because of their sizeable outsourcing sectors. Early
evidence points to job transformation and augmentation rather
than widespread displacement.
In the Philippines, employment in information and

communications, professional services, and scientiﬁc and technical
services has grown by around 4.5% over the past year. Recent
readings remain close to long-term trends. Digitally delivered
services exports continue to expand, even as the composition
shifts. Financial services and computer services are increasingly
driving growth, while the share of more traditional business process
outsourcing activities becomes less dominant.
The evidence from India points in a similar direction. Rather than

widespread layoﬀs, AI's impact appears to be showing up through
slower hiring and changing skill requirements. While AI is reducing
demand for some traditional roles, it’s creating demand for new
skills. Traditional IT ﬁrms are hiring more cautiously, but global
capability centres (GCCs) continue to expand and absorb higher-
skilled talent. As a result, India's business services exports have
more than doubled as a share of GDP since the pandemic. Even as

AI automates more routine coding and back-oﬃce tasks, demand
remains strong for higher-value activities such as software
development, analytics, AI integration and cybersecurity.
AI is already contributing to higher inﬂation
in Korea

How are Asia's trade and investment
relationships shifting?
Diversiﬁcation, not deglobalisation
The key lesson from 2026 is that tariﬀs and geopolitical tensions
are reshaping trade patterns rather than materially reducing
volumes. Rather than exiting Asia, ﬁrms have responded by
diversifying production, sourcing, and investment across a wider
range of regional economies.

Tariﬀs remain an important risk, particularly if the Trump
administration refocuses on trade policy. Recent experience
suggests higher tariﬀs are more likely to shift where production
occurs than to reverse the broader diversiﬁcation trend. While
economies such as Vietnam and Thailand may face greater
scrutiny after gaining signiﬁcant US market share, tariﬀ
diﬀerentials with China are likely to remain large enough to keep
incentives for ﬁrms to maintain diversiﬁed regional supply chains.

### Page 11

Diversiﬁcation does not mean decoupling from China
Despite tariﬀs, Asia ex-China's trade surplus with the US has almost
doubled since the end of 2021, driven primarily by Taiwan, Vietnam
and Thailand. Over the same period, much of the rise in ASEAN’s
trade surplus with the US was mirrored by a corresponding decline
in China’s surplus, pointing to a reconﬁguration rather than a
reduction of regional trade ﬂows. The pattern suggests supply
chains are being reconﬁgured across Asia rather than shifting away

from the region altogether.
More importantly, production relocation has not meaningfully
reduced China's role in regional supply chains. While ﬁnal assembly
and exports to the US increasingly take place in economies such as
Vietnam and Thailand, a signiﬁcant share of intermediate inputs
still originates in China. This means supply chains are becoming
more geographically diversiﬁed but remain closely integrated with
China.
Europe is becoming a more important trade and investment

partner
Asia's trade and investment relationships are becoming more
diversiﬁed, with Europe playing an increasingly important role.
Trade ties have strengthened signiﬁcantly in recent years, and Asia
continues to gain export market share in Europe. Since 2024,
ASEAN exports to the EU have risen by around 30%. ASEAN now
exports more to Europe than Korea, Taiwan and Japan combined.
Looking ahead, a renewed push towards free-trade agreements
could provide further support. The proposed India-EU Free Trade

Agreement is particularly signiﬁcant, while a growing network of
agreements between ASEAN economies and European partners is
helping to lower barriers to trade and investment.
The investment relationship with Europe is strengthening even
faster. EU foreign direct investment into ASEAN surged to $31 billion
in 2021, overtaking the US as ASEAN's second-largest source of FDI.
More importantly, investment decisions are increasingly shaped by
strategic imperatives rather than cost competitiveness alone.

European ﬁrms increasingly view ASEAN not just as a

and storage.

Has inﬂation peaked in Asia, and what
are the key risks to 2027?
Inﬂation outlook 2027: Lower headline gains but stickier core

pressures
Though it’s too early to call a peak in inﬂation in most of Asia, we
do expect prices to moderate in many parts of the region in 2027,
driven by easing energy and food prices. The largest declines
should occur in economies such as the Philippines and Singapore,
where recent inﬂation was driven primarily by fuel and food shocks.
Economies at the centre of the AI hardware ecosystem, particularly
Korea and Taiwan, may experience stickier core inﬂation as

technology-related price pressures continue to build.
While headline inﬂation should move lower, the balance of risks is
shifting from commodity-driven inﬂation towards technology-
driven inﬂation. This implies that while goods inﬂation may
moderate as energy and food pressures fade, core inﬂation could
remain stickier than expected. As a result, inﬂation may prove more

### Page 13

persistent than historical relationships with energy and food prices
alone would suggest.
Energy and food disinﬂation should drive the decline in inﬂation
Inﬂation has likely not yet peaked across most Asian economies,
but we expect it to do so in 4Q26-1Q27, with a clearer moderation
emerging from 2Q27. The key driver is energy prices.
We expect Brent crude to average around US$77/bbl in 2027,
around 14% below 2026 levels. While occasional US-Iran tensions

should keep some geopolitical risk premium in the market, we
expect a growing supply surplus to outweigh these concerns
provided there are no major disruptions to Persian Gulf exports. A
US-Iran agreement would present further downside risk to oil prices
and, by extension, inﬂation across Asia. Reﬁned fuel prices should
moderate year on year. Diesel prices could decline more sharply as
crack spreads normalise following the return of Russian supply,

although margins are likely to remain above historical averages.
Inﬂation should ease more noticeably from the second quarter of
2027, aided by favorable energy base eﬀects as oil prices were
unusually high a year earlier. Lower oil prices should also gradually
ﬁlter through to retail fuel costs, helping push headline inﬂation
lower across much of developing Asia.
The disinﬂationary impact should be most pronounced in oil-
importing economies such as the Philippines and Singapore, where

fuel has been a major driver of recent inﬂation pressures. In
Singapore, fuel prices accounted for almost 90% of the YoY increase
in headline CPI since February 2026. In Australia, transport costs
accounted for more than 100% of the increase in headline inﬂation,
oﬀset by weakness elsewhere in the CPI basket. In the Philippines,
fuel accounted for roughly 60% of the direct increase in inﬂation.
The overall impact is likely larger once second-round eﬀects

through transport, services and other fuel-sensitive categories are
taken into account. As lower oil prices feed through, these
economies should experience some of the largest declines in
inﬂation across the region.

Food should provide a second source of disinﬂation in 2027. As El
Niño fades in the second half, weather-related pressures on
agricultural production should ease. More broadly, demand and
supply conditions in key food markets appear more balanced than
in recent years, supporting a gradual moderation in food price
inﬂation.
The key wildcard is AI-related inﬂation. If these price increases
persist, second-round eﬀects could become more important
through services costs and wages in technology-intensive sectors.

Lower oil prices should help ease inﬂation,
while core pressures could persist
Source: CEIC
Are food price risks from EL Niño behind
us?
Probably not. The current El Niño is expected to remain strong to
very strong through 1Q27, and there remains a meaningful risk that
weather-related food price pressures persist into next year.
That said, a severe El Niño does not automatically translate into
higher inﬂation. Historical experience is mixed. During both the

2015-16 and 2023-24 El Niño episodes, headline inﬂation across
most of Asia was little changed, with only limited evidence of a
broad-based inﬂation shock. Taiwan was a notable exception in
2015-16. A key reason was that other commodity prices,
particularly oil and fertiliser, remained relatively subdued and were
generally declining during both episodes.
Your privacy is important

### Page 15

The inﬂation impact of El Niño is likely to depend as much on
energy prices as on weather conditions themselves. A sustained
decline in oil prices would not only lower fuel inﬂation directly, but
also reduce transport, fertiliser and production costs, helping to
limit second-round eﬀects on food prices.
This matters particularly for economies such as the Philippines,
where food carries a large weight in the CPI basket. Our estimates
suggest a 20% decline in fuel prices could lower headline inﬂation

by around 100bp. Evidence from the current episode suggests a
mixed and less alarming impact across food commodities. While
rice prices have remained relatively contained in both the
Philippines and India, wheat and vegetable oil prices have risen
more noticeably. Importantly, the latter appear to reﬂect
geopolitical disruptions and broader supply constraints as much as
El Niño-related eﬀects.
Food price risks in 2027 nevertheless remain skewed to the upside.

Looking ahead, global wheat production is projected to decline by
around 3% in 2026/27, reﬂecting weaker harvests in major
producers including the US, Australia and Argentina. Rice output is
also expected to decline by 2% in 2026/27, the ﬁrst annual decline
in a decade, as El Niño weighs on harvests in major Asian
exporters. Nevertheless, energy prices are likely to remain the
dominant driver of the inﬂation outlook. Food inﬂation risks could
delay the pace of disinﬂation in some economies, but lower oil

prices should still be the primary force behind headline inﬂation
moderation across most of developing Asia in 2027.
El Nino risks persist, but the inﬂation impact
remains limited and uneven

What does all this mean for central
banks?
If we’re right about fuel prices correcting, most Asian central banks
should gain conﬁdence that inﬂation is moving sustainably back
towards target, opening the door to policy easing in the second half
of 2027 to support growth. Japan remains the key exception. Unlike
elsewhere in the region, its inﬂation is increasingly being driven by
stronger wage growth, labour shortages and ﬁrmer inﬂation

expectations. This should keep the Bank of Japan on a tightening
path for longer than the rest of the region.
Growth slowdown should strengthen the case for rate cuts in
some economies
Real policy rates are near the upper end of historical ranges across
much of Asia, with the notable exceptions of the Philippines and
Taiwan. High real rates matter because they increase borrowing
costs and tend to weigh on consumption, investment and credit
growth.

Growth is already slowing in several economies. Lagged eﬀects of
past monetary tightening could weigh on activity into 2027.
Despite still-negative real rates, the Philippines has seen growth
moderate following an aggressive tightening cycle, while Australia
is likely to face a softer growth backdrop. Higher Australian interest
rates have weighed on household demand and are beginning to
feed through to the housing market. Falling house prices there

could further constrain consumption through negative wealth
eﬀects, while weaker housing activity may weigh on investment

### Page 17

and government revenues. Australia’s labour market has also
started to show clearer signs of cooling.
Against this backdrop, Australia and the Philippines are the two
economies where we see the strongest case for rate cuts in the
second half of 2027 as policymakers shift focus from inﬂation to
supporting growth.
The case for easing is less compelling in North Asia
By contrast, growth in Korea and Taiwan is likely to moderate as the
AI-driven upswing matures and overheating concerns recede. Even

so, inﬂation should remain close to target, and economic activity
should remain relatively resilient. As a result, policy easing is less
urgent than in economies such as Australia and the Philippines.
FX considerations could delay easing in India and Indonesia
FX stability may limit the scope for rate cuts in India and Indonesia.
Unlike some regional peers, the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has
historically been reluctant to use interest rates to defend the rupee.

However, policymakers recently signalled that rates could help
support currency stability if needed. While India's growth and
inﬂation backdrop should balance better in 2027, with inﬂation
closer to target and growth moderating gradually, FX management
is an important wildcard. Given the relatively shallow hiking cycle,
we don’t expect the RBI to come under signiﬁcant pressure to
reverse course and cut rates.
Indonesia faces a diﬀerent challenge. The rupiah remains one of the

region's most interest-rate-sensitive currencies. We expect it to
remain among Asia’s weakest currencies in 2027. Even if growth
softens and inﬂation stays manageable, Bank Indonesia (BI) may
be more cautious about easing policy. Maintaining a suﬃciently
attractive rate diﬀerential and limiting FX volatility will likely remain
important policy considerations, suggesting BI may lag other
central banks in the easing cycle.
Inﬂation likely to fall close to central bank

targets by 4Q27

Can tech-linked currencies maintain their
leadership in 2027?
FX outlook 2027: Rotation underway, but tech currencies should
continue to lead
Asian currencies have had a mixed run in 2026. AI-linked currencies
such as the KRW, CNY, SGD and MYR generally outperformed, while
the INR, IDR and PHP weakened amid softer growth, higher energy
prices and domestic challenges. One notable exception among the
tech-linked economies is the Taiwanese dollar, which depreciated

by around 5% despite strong current account balances, as a tech-
sector sell-oﬀ in recent months triggered equity outﬂows.
In 2027, lower oil prices and a softer dollar should provide a more
supportive backdrop for Asian FX. However, we think current
account balances and capital ﬂows are likely to play a larger role
than interest-rate diﬀerentials in driving FX performance. While
some rotation into South Asian currencies is already underway, we
continue to expect tech-linked currencies, particularly the SGD in

Southeast Asia and the KRW and TWD in North Asia, to outperform.
This is thanks to strong current account surpluses and exporter FX
conversion, which provide ongoing support. By contrast, currencies
with weaker external positions and less stable capital inﬂows
remain more vulnerable.
Current account surpluses trump rate diﬀerentials in 2027
Rate diﬀerentials are becoming less supportive for EM Asia. Most
Asian central banks are nearing the end of their tightening cycles

and are likely to prepare for policy easing during 2027. Interest-rate

support for Asian currencies should gradually diminish, particularly
for rate-sensitive currencies such as the IDR.
Instead, current account balances and capital ﬂows are likely to
play a larger role in driving FX performance. The CNY and KRW have
been among the strongest performers since the US-Iran conﬂict in
February 2026, appreciating despite a relatively resilient DXY. This
suggests domestic fundamentals are playing a larger role in driving
Asian FX than broad dollar movements.

We remain bullish on the KRW, SGD and TWD. The AI cycle may be
plateauing, but activity remains high. Strong AI-related exports
should continue to support current account surpluses, while
exporter FX conversion should help oﬀset capital outﬂows. In Korea,
sizeable equity outﬂows since September have not prevented KRW
appreciation, highlighting the importance of exporter conversion
and capital repatriation. Measures to encourage domestic
investment and improve shareholder returns could further support

the KRW in 2027. Similarly, Japan's eﬀorts to improve domestic
returns and shareholder distributions could gradually slow capital
outﬂows over time. So, currencies backed by large current account
surpluses, particularly the KRW, TWD and CNY, should be relatively
well supported even as rate diﬀerentials become less favourable.
The main risk to this view is a sharper-than-expected slowdown in
the AI cycle, which could weaken export earnings and reduce

current account support for these currencies.
Rotation towards South Asia should continue, but selectively
Post-midterms, there’s been a rotation from North Asian to South
Asian currencies. The tech sell-oﬀ and lower oil prices have
improved valuations across parts of South Asia. Falling energy
prices should continue to support oil-importing currencies such as
the INR, IDR and PHP.
However, tighter global ﬁnancial conditions mean capital will not be

distributed evenly. We remain more constructive on the INR, as
growth should remain relatively resilient, supported by supply-
chain diversiﬁcation and continued FDI inﬂows. By contrast,

dominant driver of FX performance, with current account dynamics
and capital ﬂows playing a larger role.

Will resident demand for foreign assets

continue to constrain Asian FX?
Resident demand for foreign assets remains a key reason why
stronger external balances aren’t translating into sustained Asian
FX appreciation. Current account surpluses remain supportive for
several Asian currencies.
Two structural forces are at work. First, resident demand for foreign
assets continues to rise as retail and institutional investors, along
with corporates, seek greater diversiﬁcation and access to higher

### Page 21

returns abroad. Second, outbound direct investment remains
elevated as ﬁrms reposition supply chains and expand overseas
operations. These trends are structural and unlikely to reverse
quickly.
Taiwan provides a clear example. Corporates continue to diversify
production outside China, with investment increasingly directed
towards the US and Southeast Asia. The sharp rise in outward direct
investment in 2026 reﬂects long-term supply-chain repositioning.

While these outﬂows have weighed on the TWD, the drag could
lessen if the AI cycle moderates in 2026, reducing the need for
overseas capex.
Japan remains another important source of regional capital
outﬂows. Domestic institutional investors, including pension funds
and investment trusts, remain net buyers of foreign assets as part
of long-term portfolio diversiﬁcation strategies. While some life
insurers reduced unhedged foreign bond holdings in August and

September as the yen appreciated, the broader trend remains
overseas allocation. This has been a key constraint on JPY
appreciation. While potential capital repatriation has drawn greater
scrutiny since Tokyo moved to stabilise the currency after its sharp
drop earlier this year, concerns over Japan’s ﬁscal outlook and
government bond supply have dampened appetite for large-scale
inﬂows.
Korea illustrates why capital outﬂows don’t necessarily translate

into FX weakness. Residents (including retail investors and the
National Pension Scheme) continue to invest heavily abroad, with
equity outﬂows annualising at around $100bn. Corporates have
also increased overseas asset accumulation through trade credit
and oﬀshore FX deposits. However, Korea's largely AI-driven current
account surplus and strong exporter FX conversion will likely be
suﬃcient to oﬀset these outﬂows, supporting our KRW
appreciation view.

India has emerged as a growing source of capital outﬂows, with
annual outward FDI more than doubling since 2024 as ﬁrms deploy
capital overseas rather than domestically. While recent data

Taken together, resident demand for foreign assets is likely to
remain a structural constraint on Asian FX appreciation in 2027.
Strong current account balances can still support currencies,

How vulnerable is Asia to a further rise
in global bond yields?
Asia is not immune, but better positioned than developed markets
Amid the sharp rise in advanced-economy bond yields and the US-
Iran conﬂict, Asian rates have become more sensitive to US rates in
2026. Looking ahead, Asia is likely to remain exposed to higher

global yields as US yields and term premia stay elevated, reﬂecting
worsening ﬁscal positions among major sovereign issuers and
increased debt supply associated with AI-related investment.
Higher global rates would raise debt-servicing costs and put further
pressure on ﬁscal balances across the region. However, Asian
sovereigns are generally better positioned than developed market
peers to absorb a period of higher borrowing costs. Public debt
burdens are typically lower, reliance on foreign investors is generally

Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 23

smaller, and debt maturities are often longer, particularly relative to
Europe. This means higher rates should feed through to
government interest expenses more gradually.
In addition, many Asian economies beneﬁt from strong domestic
savings and deep local investor bases. In several cases, solid
external positions provide an important buﬀer against tighter
global ﬁnancial conditions.
Foreign ownership remains a key vulnerability in some markets
Not all economies are equally insulated. Foreign ownership of

government debt remains relatively high in markets such as
Indonesia and the Philippines, making them more sensitive to shifts
in global risk appetite. As a result, a further rise in global yields
could have a larger impact on domestic bond markets and
ﬁnancing costs in these economies.
Japan is an outlier, but near-term risks remain manageable
Japan remains an outlier in gross public debt, estimated at around
205% of GDP in 2026 by the IMF. However, broader measures that

net out government cash, deposits, and other ﬁnancial assets —
such as loans and debt securities — reduce net debt to roughly
137% of GDP, according to the IMF.
In the near term, Japan retains signiﬁcant buﬀers. The government
bond market is deep and liquid, debt maturities are long, debt is
issued almost entirely in yen, and substantial public-sector assets
provide an additional cushion. These factors should help limit near-
term funding risks even if global yields remain elevated.

The longer-term challenge is diﬀerent. Japan’s rising age-related
spending and higher interest costs will continue to strain public
ﬁnances, making ﬁscal sustainability increasingly dependent on
stronger nominal growth.
Near-term risks to Asia debt remain
manageable

### Page 24

Source: CEIC, IMF
Conclusion
In a capital-scarce world, Asia stands out by attracting a growing
share of global FDI and remaining at the centre of the AI supply
chain, two forces that continue to support the region's external
balances.
However, tighter global ﬁnancial conditions mean capital will
become increasingly selective. In FX, we remain constructive on the
KRW and TWD, where strong AI-related exports should continue to
support current account balances and exporter FX conversion

should help oﬀset capital outﬂows. Measures to encourage
repatriation of overseas assets could provide additional tailwind.
We also favour the INR due to resilient growth, supply-chain
diversiﬁcation, and steady FDI inﬂows.
For rates, moderating inﬂation gives most Asian central banks
scope to ease policy in 2H27, with Australia and the Philippines
oﬀering the strongest cases for rate cuts to support growth. Japan
remains the key outlier. Stronger wage growth and more persistent

inﬂation raise the risk of a more hawkish-than-expected BoJ,
particularly if domestic demand continues to strengthen. The Bank
of Korea, meanwhile, could deliver a surprise cut if the K-shaped
growth dynamic widens and domestic growth weakens further.
Overall, we expect growth leadership to broaden as the AI cycle
matures. While AI-related sectors and currencies should continue to
outperform, lower energy prices, resilient domestic demand and
ongoing supply-chain diversiﬁcation should narrow the gap

between the region's winners and laggards.

## Document 346: Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP.  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

another hike in March, but either way we see renewed cuts in late 2027/early 2028.

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

### Page 6

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP, and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP, although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.

Exhibit 8: UK Growth Forecasts Have Drifted Higher

Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   6
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 7

10. Our strongest global view is that central banks will deliver fewer hikes than currently 
priced.  In addition, our rates strategists flag underweight positioning, diminished 
technical risks, and risk asset pressures as reasons why the recent rally might extend.  
The market’s narrow breadth makes our equity strategists wary of momentum factor 
exposure, but they still expect the S&P 500 to resume its climb after the midterm 
election, as earnings continue to grow solidly.  Our credit strategists remain cautious on 
IG technology bonds but see scope for some near-term reversal in HY given the scale of 
the recent selloff.  Our FX strategists expect the Japanese yen to act as a hedge against 
further AI jitters, in part because such jitters would shrink US-Japan interest rate 
differentials.  And our commodity strategists recommend targeted exposures to oil 
products and European natural gas to hedge near-term escalation risks alongside 
longer-term exposures to copper, gold, and select power markets to benefit from 
structural supply/demand imbalances. 
Jan Hatzius

Editorial
Tactical plays within a diversified allocation 
Strategy summary: Our calls to dial down risk since early 
October have helped mitigate a bear market in semiconductors 
and the sharpest single-day post-mid-term election downturn in 
US equities in over three decades. Volatility has eased after the 
post-election spike, helping the IT sector partly recover. We see 
further upside in our opportunistic US IT and healthcare sector 
equity ideas. In bonds, we lock in gains on US inflation-
protected bonds as inflation nears a peak.

mid-cycle market dynamics. Last week, as the US equity 
market volatility index VIX surged above 25, we partially dialled 
up risk with a tactical idea on the more diversified US IT sector. 
Staying tactical within a diversified allocation. Equity 
volatility following last week’s Democratic clean sweep in the 
US elections has subsided. Questions about the pace of AI data 
centre buildout are likely to linger until Democrats take office in 
in January. Nvidia’s turmoil adds uncertainty. We remain 
broadly diversified and balanced in our core allocation across 
assets, sector and regions, while seeking tactical opportunities. 
History suggests sharp selloffs are followed by near-term 
volatility, particularly during macroeconomic stress. However, 
the balance of historical outcomes has typically shifted strongly 
to the upside over a three- to six-month horizon. Besides, 
historically, US equity markets have delivered the strongest 12-
month returns after opposition clean sweeps in the mid-terms. 
Rotation driving US IT sector recovery: As volatility eases, 
our opportunistic US IT equities idea, launched a week ago, has 
performed well, despite some significantly negative company 
specific developments. Attractive valuations support further 
upside. Investors are rotating within IT from semiconductors, 
affected by concerns over the pace of AI data centre buildout 
and Nvidia’s troubles, towards software and hardware 
providers. The latter have strong cash flows and balance sheets 
and are likely to benefit from AI monetisation. Together, they 
account for more than 60% of the IT sector (see page 4).
